Well, I suppose I do... just a little bit. I mean, I certainly don't want to seem vain or obsessed with it, but before I leave the house, I might just glance at it in the hall mirror. You know, just a few times to see how it looks in the light.

Does that sound terribly neurotic of me? I just want to look presentable, and I don't want to make anyone uncomfortable if they happen to see it. I hope that's a normal thing to do.

I'm very capable of getting my work done and taking care of the house. \n\nI suppose... well, maybe I do feel a tiny bit embarrassed sometimes, if I think someone is looking at it. I try to just brush it off and keep a smile on my face. \n\nIt's completely normal to want to put your best foot forward, isn't it? Well, I wouldn't call it a habit, exactly, and I certainly try not to make a fuss over it. But... I suppose I do check it a little bit before I head out.

I keep feeling like everyone’s eyes go right to it. You know, just a few times to see how it looks in the light. \n\nAnd I have started wearing longer sleeves more often, or I'll try to find a nice bracelet that sits just right over it. It's nothing too drastic, I promise! Just... little adjustments. Sometimes I'll ask my husband if he thinks it looks obvious, just to be sure. He always tells me it's barely there and that I'm being silly, which I know I probably am. \n\nDoes that sound terribly neurotic of me? I just want to look presentable, and I don't want to make anyone uncomfortable if they happen to see it. It's nothing too drastic, I promise! Just... little adjustments. Sometimes I'll ask my husband if he thinks it looks obvious, just to be sure. He always tells me it's barely there and that I'm being silly, which I know I probably am. \n\nDoes that sound terribly neurotic of me? I just want to look presentable, and I don't want to make anyone uncomfortable if they happen to see it. I hope that's a normal thing to do.\n\nDoctor: Do these worries about the scar ever make it hard for you to enjoy social events, or do you find yourself avoiding situations where it might be seen?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say I avoid things entirely... I mean, I still try to keep up with everything. But, um, I suppose... well, I guess it has caused a few little hiccups lately. \n\nIt's just that sometimes, getting ready takes a bit longer than I expect. I'll be trying to get my sleeves to sit right, or picking out the right jewelry, and... well, I have been a little late turning in some of my bookkeeping work recently. And I might have missed a choir practice or two, and maybe a theater volunteer shift. \n\nBut it's not because I don't want to be there! I really love volunteering and singing. It's just... I get so caught up trying to make sure I look tidy and that my wrist won't be a distraction to anyone else. I just want to be sure I'm not making a spectacle of myself, you know? \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound terribly irresponsible. I really am trying my best to balance it all. Does that make sense, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have these worries about your wrist ever caused you significant distress or affected your ability to function in your daily life, such as difficulty concentrating, feeling embarrassed, or feeling upset for long periods of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say it's caused any real distress. I mean, certainly nothing that would stop me from functioning! I'm very capable of getting my work done and taking care of the house. \n\nI suppose... well, maybe I do feel a tiny bit embarrassed sometimes, if I think someone is looking at it. And maybe it crosses my mind a bit more than it should. But I wouldn't say I'm upset for long periods of time or anything like that. I try to just brush it off and keep a smile on my face. \n\nIt's completely normal to want to put your best foot forward, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting or making a mountain out of a molehill. I really do try to be sensible about these things.\n\nDoctor: When you're in social situations, like church or bridge club, and you're worried others might notice the scar, do you feel anxious or self-conscious, even if you try to brush it off?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I do get a little self-conscious, yes. I try very hard not to let it show, of course! I'll keep smiling and chatting, but... \n\nIt's just, I know the scar on my wrist is probably not as bad as it looks to me. My husband tells me that all the time. But when I'm sitting there at church, or dealing cards at bridge club, I just... I keep feeling like everyone's eyes go right to it. It almost feels like it's the very first thing they notice about me now, instead of my face or what I'm saying. \n\nI try to just tuck my hand under the table or keep my sleeve pulled down so I don't bother anyone with it. I know it's silly to worry so much about what other people think, but... it's just human nature to want to make a good impression, right? I hope you don't think I'm being terribly vain for worrying about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly adjusting your sleeves, checking your reflection, or doing anything else to make sure the scar isn't noticeable, even if it's just out of habit?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't call it a habit, exactly, and I certainly try not to make a fuss over it. But... I suppose I do check it a little bit before I head out. \n\nJust... you know, when I'm walking past the hall mirror, I might glance at my wrist a few times. And I might try on a couple of different blouses to see which sleeve sits better, or maybe slip on a bracelet just to be sure it's covered nicely. \n\nAnd, well, sometimes I'll ask my husband how it looks before we leave. He's so sweet, he always tells me it's barely even there and that I look fine. I just like to double-check, you know? Just to be absolutely sure everything is neat and tidy. That's a perfectly normal thing for a lady to do before she goes out, wouldn't you say?\n\nDoctor: When you're in situations where your wrist might be exposed, does it cause you a lot of anxiety or worry, even if you try to hide it?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say it causes a *lot* of anxiety. I mean, I certainly don't let it ruin my day or anything like that. I'm very good at keeping a positive attitude! \n\nI suppose... well, maybe I feel a tiny bit on edge if I'm wearing short sleeves and someone is sitting very close to me. I might just quietly fold my hands in my lap, just to be polite, you know? I wouldn't want to make anyone else uncomfortable by having it out in the open. \n\nBut it's not a terrible worry. Just a little flutter, really. I just like things to be pleasant for everyone. That's understandable, isn't it?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Well, a *strong* need? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I don't want you to think I'm... well, obsessing over it. I certainly don't think about going to a plastic surgeon to have it fixed or anything drastic like that! 

I just... I suppose I just prefer to keep it covered while I'm at the office doing the books, or when I'm out with friends. It's just a little adjustment, really. I hope that's a normal thing to do.\n\nDoctor: Do these worries about the scar ever make it hard for you to enjoy social events, or do you find yourself avoiding situations where it might be seen?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say I avoid things entirely... I mean, I still try to keep up with everything. But, um, I suppose... well, I guess it has caused a few little hiccups lately. \n\nIt's just that sometimes, getting ready takes a bit longer than I expect. I'll be trying to get my sleeves to sit right, or picking out the right jewelry, and... well, I have been a little late turning in some of my bookkeeping work recently. And I might have missed a choir practice or two, and maybe a theater volunteer shift. \n\nBut it's not because I don't want to be there! I really love volunteering and singing. It's just... I get so caught up trying to make sure I look tidy and that my wrist won't be a distraction to anyone else. I just want to be sure I'm not making a spectacle of myself, you know? \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound terribly irresponsible. I really am trying my best to balance it all. Does that make sense, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have these worries about your wrist ever caused you significant distress or affected your ability to function in your daily life, such as difficulty concentrating, feeling embarrassed, or feeling upset for long periods of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say it's caused any real distress. I mean, certainly nothing that would stop me from functioning! I'm very capable of getting my work done and taking care of the house. \n\nI suppose... well, maybe I do feel a tiny bit embarrassed sometimes, if I think someone is looking at it. And maybe it crosses my mind a bit more than it should. But I wouldn't say I'm upset for long periods of time or anything like that. I try to just brush it off and keep a smile on my face. \n\nIt's completely normal to want to put your best foot forward, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting or making a mountain out of a molehill. I really do try to be sensible about these things.\n\nDoctor: When you're in social situations, like church or bridge club, and you're worried others might notice the scar, do you feel anxious or self-conscious, even if you try to brush it off?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I do get a little self-conscious, yes. I try very hard not to let it show, of course! I'll keep smiling and chatting, but... \n\nIt's just, I know the scar on my wrist is probably not as bad as it looks to me. My husband tells me that all the time. But when I'm sitting there at church, or dealing cards at bridge club, I just... I keep feeling like everyone's eyes go right to it. It almost feels like it's the very first thing they notice about me now, instead of my face or what I'm saying. \n\nI try to just tuck my hand under the table or keep my sleeve pulled down so I don't bother anyone with it. I know it's silly to worry so much about what other people think, but... it's just human nature to want to make a good impression, right? I hope you don't think I'm being terribly vain for worrying about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly adjusting your sleeves, checking your reflection, or doing anything else to make sure the scar isn't noticeable, even if it's just out of habit?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't call it a habit, exactly, and I certainly try not to make a fuss over it. But... I suppose I do check it a little bit before I head out. \n\nJust... you know, when I'm walking past the hall mirror, I might glance at my wrist a few times. And I might try on a couple of different blouses to see which sleeve sits better, or maybe slip on a bracelet just to be sure it's covered nicely. \n\nAnd, well, sometimes I'll ask my husband how it looks before we leave. He's so sweet, he always tells me it's barely even there and that I look fine. I just like to double-check, you know? Just to be absolutely sure everything is neat and tidy. That's a perfectly normal thing for a lady to do before she goes out, wouldn't you say?\n\nDoctor: When you're in situations where your wrist might be exposed, does it cause you a lot of anxiety or worry, even if you try to hide it?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say it causes a *lot* of anxiety. I mean, I certainly don't let it ruin my day or anything like that. I'm very good at keeping a positive attitude! \n\nI suppose... well, maybe I feel a tiny bit on edge if I'm wearing short sleeves and someone is sitting very close to me. I might just quietly fold my hands in my lap, just to be polite, you know? I wouldn't want to make anyone else uncomfortable by having it out in the open. \n\nBut it's not a terrible worry. Just a little flutter, really. I just like things to be pleasant for everyone. That's understandable, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a strong need to fix or hide the scar, even if you know it might not make a big difference, just to feel a bit more at ease?\n\nPatient: Well, a *strong* need? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I don't want you to think I'm... well, obsessing over it. I certainly don't think about going to a plastic surgeon to have it fixed or anything drastic like that! \n\nI just... I suppose I just prefer to keep it covered while I'm at the office doing the books, or when I'm out with friends. It's just a little adjustment, really. Just pulling a cardigan sleeve down or wearing a nice watch. It's not a desperate feeling at all, just... just wanting to look my best and not draw the wrong kind of attention. \n\nI'm sure you see ladies in here who worry far, far more than I do, right? I try to be very sensible about it. I'm not making too much of a fuss, am I?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Oh, heavens no. When I'm just at home by myself, or with my husband, I don't sit around fretting over it. That would be a bit silly, wouldn't it?

I mean, I certainly don't want to seem vain or obsessed with it, but before I leave the house, I might just glance at it in the hall mirror. You know, just a few times to see how it looks in the light. \n\nAnd I have started wearing longer sleeves more often, or I'll try to find a nice bracelet that sits just right over it. It's nothing too drastic, I promise! Just... little adjustments. Sometimes I'll ask my husband if he thinks it looks obvious, just to be sure. He always tells me it's barely there and that I'm being silly, which I know I probably am. \n\nDoes that sound terribly neurotic of me? I just want to look presentable, and I don't want to make anyone uncomfortable if they happen to see it. I hope that's a normal thing to do.\n\nDoctor: Do these worries about the scar ever make it hard for you to enjoy social events, or do you find yourself avoiding situations where it might be seen?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say I avoid things entirely... I mean, I still try to keep up with everything. But, um, I suppose... well, I guess it has caused a few little hiccups lately. \n\nIt's just that sometimes, getting ready takes a bit longer than I expect. I'll be trying to get my sleeves to sit right, or picking out the right jewelry, and... well, I have been a little late turning in some of my bookkeeping work recently. And I might have missed a choir practice or two, and maybe a theater volunteer shift. \n\nBut it's not because I don't want to be there! I really love volunteering and singing. It's just... I get so caught up trying to make sure I look tidy and that my wrist won't be a distraction to anyone else. I just want to be sure I'm not making a spectacle of myself, you know? \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound terribly irresponsible. I really am trying my best to balance it all. Does that make sense, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have these worries about your wrist ever caused you significant distress or affected your ability to function in your daily life, such as difficulty concentrating, feeling embarrassed, or feeling upset for long periods of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say it's caused any real distress. I mean, certainly nothing that would stop me from functioning! I'm very capable of getting my work done and taking care of the house. \n\nI suppose... well, maybe I do feel a tiny bit embarrassed sometimes, if I think someone is looking at it. And maybe it crosses my mind a bit more than it should. But I wouldn't say I'm upset for long periods of time or anything like that. I try to just brush it off and keep a smile on my face. \n\nIt's completely normal to want to put your best foot forward, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting or making a mountain out of a molehill. I really do try to be sensible about these things.\n\nDoctor: When you're in social situations, like church or bridge club, and you're worried others might notice the scar, do you feel anxious or self-conscious, even if you try to brush it off?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I do get a little self-conscious, yes. I try very hard not to let it show, of course! I'll keep smiling and chatting, but... \n\nIt's just, I know the scar on my wrist is probably not as bad as it looks to me. My husband tells me that all the time. But when I'm sitting there at church, or dealing cards at bridge club, I just... I keep feeling like everyone's eyes go right to it. It almost feels like it's the very first thing they notice about me now, instead of my face or what I'm saying. \n\nI try to just tuck my hand under the table or keep my sleeve pulled down so I don't bother anyone with it. I know it's silly to worry so much about what other people think, but... it's just human nature to want to make a good impression, right? I hope you don't think I'm being terribly vain for worrying about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly adjusting your sleeves, checking your reflection, or doing anything else to make sure the scar isn't noticeable, even if it's just out of habit?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't call it a habit, exactly, and I certainly try not to make a fuss over it. But... I suppose I do check it a little bit before I head out. \n\nJust... you know, when I'm walking past the hall mirror, I might glance at my wrist a few times. And I might try on a couple of different blouses to see which sleeve sits better, or maybe slip on a bracelet just to be sure it's covered nicely. \n\nAnd, well, sometimes I'll ask my husband how it looks before we leave. He's so sweet, he always tells me it's barely even there and that I look fine. I just like to double-check, you know? Just to be absolutely sure everything is neat and tidy. That's a perfectly normal thing for a lady to do before she goes out, wouldn't you say?\n\nDoctor: When you're in situations where your wrist might be exposed, does it cause you a lot of anxiety or worry, even if you try to hide it?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say it causes a *lot* of anxiety. I mean, I certainly don't let it ruin my day or anything like that. I'm very good at keeping a positive attitude! \n\nI suppose... well, maybe I feel a tiny bit on edge if I'm wearing short sleeves and someone is sitting very close to me. I might just quietly fold my hands in my lap, just to be polite, you know? I wouldn't want to make anyone else uncomfortable by having it out in the open. \n\nBut it's not a terrible worry. Just a little flutter, really. I just like things to be pleasant for everyone. That's understandable, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a strong need to fix or hide the scar, even if you know it might not make a big difference, just to feel a bit more at ease?\n\nPatient: Well, a *strong* need? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I don't want you to think I'm... well, obsessing over it. I certainly don't think about going to a plastic surgeon to have it fixed or anything drastic like that! \n\nI just... I suppose I just prefer to keep it covered while I'm at the office doing the books, or when I'm out with friends. It's just a little adjustment, really. Just pulling a cardigan sleeve down or wearing a nice watch. It's not a desperate feeling at all, just... just wanting to look my best and not draw the wrong kind of attention. \n\nI'm sure you see ladies in here who worry far, far more than I do, right? I try to be very sensible about it. I'm not making too much of a fuss, am I?\n\nDoctor: When you're alone at home, do you ever find yourself feeling anxious or upset just thinking about the scar, even when no one else is around to see it?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens no. When I'm just at home by myself, or with my husband, I don't sit around fretting over it. That would be a bit silly, wouldn't it? \n\nI mean, if I'm just doing some accounting work at the dining table, or puttering around the kitchen, I don't really give it much thought at all. It's just... well, it's just there. I might notice it if I'm washing the dishes, but it doesn't make me upset. \n\nI'm really quite a cheerful person most of the time! I certainly wouldn't let something like that bother me when I'm just relaxing in my own home. That's the normal way to feel, isn't it? Just leaving those little worries at the front door?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Oh, no, truly. It's just like I mentioned a moment ago... even if I happen to look right at it, it really doesn't bother me when it's just me.

But... I suppose I do check it a little bit before I head out. \n\nJust... you know, when I'm walking past the hall mirror, I might glance at my wrist a few times. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I wouldn't call it a terrible anxiety, nothing like that. I always make sure to be pleasant and enjoy myself! But... I suppose it is a little hard to completely ignore sometimes. 

I mean, I know the scar on my wrist is probably not nearly as bad as it looks to me. I'm sensible enough to realize that. It's just that when I'm out with people—like at church, or when I'm sitting down for my weekly bridge game—I just keep feeling like everyone's eyes go right to it. Even if I've adjusted my cuffs, I get this silly feeling that it's the very first thing they notice about me now. 

I try my absolute best to just smile and push past it, though. I certainly wouldn't want to make a fuss or make anyone else uncomfortable. It's perfectly natural to feel a bit self-conscious about a blemish when you're out with your friends, don't you think?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, distress? Goodness, no. I wouldn't say I feel... distressed. That sounds so terribly serious, doesn't it? 

If it's a very warm day, well, I usually just opt for a nice three-quarter sleeve or a light cotton cardigan. It's very sensible for the air conditioning anyway, especially when I'm sitting at my desk doing the books. And when I shake hands, I just... you know, I might just make sure my cuff is sitting right before I reach out. Just a gentle little tug. 

It's really just a habit at this point. I don't feel anxious about it, truly I don't. I just prefer to keep things looking tidy and proper so I don't draw any unnecessary attention. That makes sense, doesn't it? I think most ladies like to present themselves nicely without making a big fuss over it.

It's just... well, it's just there. I might notice it if I'm washing the dishes, but it doesn't make me upset. \n\nI'm really quite a cheerful person most of the time! I certainly wouldn't let something like that bother me when I'm just relaxing in my own home. That's the normal way to feel, isn't it? Just leaving those little worries at the front door?\n\nDoctor: When you're alone at home, and you happen to notice the scar, does it bother you or make you feel uneasy even if no one else is around?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, truly. It's just... well, it's just my wrist. I suppose I only really give it any thought when I'm worried about what other people might think, if that makes sense? \n\nBut on my own? I... I have been just a little bit distracted lately. It's terribly embarrassing to admit, but I have actually been a little late turning in some of my bookkeeping work. Just because I get sort of... caught up, you know, fussing with my sweater and making sure my sleeves are sitting exactly right while I'm working. 

And... oh, I hate to even say this, because I pride myself on being reliable, but I’ve actually skipped a few of my choir practices and my theater volunteer commitments recently. I feel just awful about letting people down! It’s just that getting ready takes me a bit longer now, trying to find the right outfit to make absolutely sure my wrist won't be noticed by anyone. Sometimes I get so caught up in fixing it that it just feels easier to stay home. 

I hope you don't think I'm an irresponsible person for that. I'm really trying my best to stay on top of things! It's just a minor little bump in the road, don't you think?

But... I suppose I do check it a little bit before I head out. \n\nJust... you know, when I'm walking past the hall mirror, I might glance at my wrist a few times. And I might try on a couple of different blouses to see which sleeve sits better, or maybe slip on a bracelet just to be sure it's covered nicely. \n\nAnd, well, sometimes I'll ask my husband how it looks before we leave. He's so sweet, he always tells me it's barely even there and that I look fine. I just like to double-check, you know? Just to be absolutely sure everything is neat and tidy. That's a perfectly normal thing for a lady to do before she goes out, wouldn't you say?\n\nDoctor: When you're in situations where your wrist might be exposed, does it cause you a lot of anxiety or worry, even if you try to hide it?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say it causes a *lot* of anxiety. I mean, I certainly don't let it ruin my day or anything like that. I'm very good at keeping a positive attitude! \n\nI suppose... well, maybe I feel a tiny bit on edge if I'm wearing short sleeves and someone is sitting very close to me. Just a little flutter, really. I just like things to be pleasant for everyone. That's understandable, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a strong need to fix or hide the scar, even if you know it might not make a big difference, just to feel a bit more at ease?\n\nPatient: Well, a *strong* need? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I don't want you to think I'm... well, obsessing over it. I certainly don't think about going to a plastic surgeon to have it fixed or anything drastic like that! \n\nI just... I suppose I just prefer to keep it covered while I'm at the office doing the books, or when I'm out with friends. It's just a little adjustment, really. Just pulling a cardigan sleeve down or wearing a nice watch. It's not a desperate feeling at all, just... just wanting to look my best and not draw the wrong kind of attention. \n\nI'm sure you see ladies in here who worry far, far more than I do, right? I try to be very sensible about it. I'm not making too much of a fuss, am I?\n\nDoctor: When you're alone at home, do you ever find yourself feeling anxious or upset just thinking about the scar, even when no one else is around to see it?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens no. When I'm just at home by myself, or with my husband, I don't sit around fretting over it. It's just... well, it's just there. I might notice it if I'm washing the dishes, but it doesn't make me upset. \n\nI'm really quite a cheerful person most of the time! It's just like I mentioned a moment ago... even if I happen to look right at it, it really doesn't bother me when it's just me. \n\nIf I catch sight of it while I'm, oh, folding the laundry or getting ready for bed, I don't feel uneasy at all. It's just... well, it's just my wrist. I suppose I only really give it any thought when I'm worried about what other people might think, if that makes sense? \n\nBut on my own? I just don't want you to think I sit around making myself miserable over it, because I certainly don't!\n\nDoctor: When you're getting ready for the day or preparing for social events, do you find that thoughts about the scar take up a lot of your time or attention, even if you try to push them aside?\n\nPatient: Well, a lot of time? Oh, I wouldn't say a *lot* of time. I don't want you to picture me standing there for hours, agonizing over it. I'm very efficient when I get ready for the day, really I am. \n\nI suppose... well, before I leave the house, I might just glance at my wrist in the hall mirror.
